| A-bench-001 | SUPERSEDED by A-cost-002 (CP2 P2a, 31 Oct 2026); was: method for converting a benchmark cost of price year *y* in currency *c* to SAR at the 2025 base (A-cost-001) | route A: deflate in the source country's currency with its GDP deflator to 2025, convert at the 2025 official exchange rate, then SAR at the peg 3.75; route B: convert at the price-year official rate, deflate in USD with the US GDP deflator, then SAR at the peg | method | both routes are printed; their spread is the conversion uncertainty. PPP conversion is the main alternative (it lowers costs from high-price countries relative to market rates) and is not applied: construction inputs such as rolling stock, systems and steel trade at market rates, while labour and land do not, so neither basis is exact | assumed. Series: World Bank WDI NY.GDP.DEFL.ZS (S-bench-idx-001) and PA.NUS.FCRF (S-bench-idx-002), last updated 2026-10-08. A GDP deflator is an economy-wide index, not a construction-cost index; a national construction-cost index would be closer but is not available on one basis for every country. Route B is not applied to euro figures dated before 1999 (the World Bank series is in legacy currencies). Route A is not available for AED (no 2025 UAE deflator in the file), TWD (Taiwan is not in WDI) or EUR figures of projects outside the euro area (WDI has no euro-area deflator); for the latter, route B uses the euro-area exchange rate (S-bench-idx-003). approval: SUPERSEDED by A-cost-002 at CP2 P2a (Tariq, 31 Oct 2026): one index, cities/ksa/costs/index.yaml; SAR items use the Saudi CPI, foreign benchmarks use the source economy's GDP deflator before conversion, then the peg. Route A is kept as the central method of A-cost-002 (the same arithmetic and WDI series); route B is replaced by the A-cost-002 sensitivity (price-year rate and peg, then Saudi CPI). index-r1b.yaml was folded in (source rows S-bench-idx-001…003 kept) and deleted; every before/after value is in docs/benchmarks/normalisation-delta.md. Not to be used for new work | assumed | none since CP2 P2a (was cities/ksa/benchmarks/index-r1b.yaml, deleted; rail/benchmarks.py; docs/benchmarks/README.md); before values in docs/benchmarks/normalisation-delta.md |

Collectors had used 2017, 2018 and "unknown" for the same table; one rule is applied to every ECA money row (coordinator, 31 Oct 2026). approval: pending CP2 | assumed | cities/ksa/benchmarks/metrics-*.csv (rows citing ECA); docs/benchmarks/README.md |

| A-cba-203 | road_crash_cost_sar_per_car_vkm | 0.1475 | SAR per car vehicle-km (average total casualty cost) | 0.0724 - 0.4385 | assumed (registered): S-r5-cba-219, S-r5-cba-215, S-r5-cba-218, S-r5-cba-206, S-r5-cba-221, S-r5-cba-216, S-soc-317, S-r5-cba-220, S-r5-cba-222, S-r5-cba-201. derivation (central): VSL = 70 x KSA GDP per capita 2025 USD 34,536.66 (S-r5-cba-215) = USD 2,417,566 = SAR 9,065,872 (iRAP rule of thumb 70, range 60-80, S-r5-cba-218). Deaths 4,555 and injuries 24,400 (GASTAT 2022, S-r5-cba-219). Injury value as a share of VSL from TAG Table A4.1.1 (S-r5-cba-206): serious 0.1112, slight 0.0085; serious share of reported injuries 0.5 (assumption; GASTAT gives no split). Total = 4,555 x 9.066M + 24,400 x (0.5 x 0.1112 + 0.5 x 0.0085) x 9.066M = SAR 54.5 bn. Share attributable to car traffic 0.8 (assumption; trucks, buses, motorcycles). VKM proxy (no KSA vehicle-km statistic found): gasoline 838 L per person 2024 (S-r5-cba-221, secondary) x population 35,300,280 (S-r5-cba-216) = 29.58 bn L (= 186.1 M bbl; press reports 188.2 M bbl) x 10 km/L (S-soc-317) = 295.8 bn vkm. 54.5 x 0.8 / 295.8 = 0.1475. Low: VSL 60x, serious share 0.3, car share 0.7, 14 km/L (414.1 bn vkm) = 0.0724. High: EU Handbook 2019 car external accident cost 7.2 EUR-cent/vkm (S-r5-cba-222) x 5.406 x 1.126 = 0.4385; the KSA derivation with VSL 80x, WHO-estimated 6,651 deaths (2021, S-r5-cba-220, secondary), all injuries serious, share 0.9, 8 km/L gives 0.369. Reported injuries (5.4 per death) look under-reported against EU ratios, so the central is likely low. Damage-only crash and admin costs excluded. Cross-check: TAG A5.4.2 rural A-road accident MEC 1.16 p/vkm (external part only, UK risk) = 0.0705 (S-r5-cba-201) | assumed | cities/ksa/assumptions/cba_externalities.yaml; economics/ (R5) |

Central = conventional; low = HSR value; high = 1.5 x conventional (assumption: level crossings and camel/sand incursion risk on desert lines; no KSA rail casualty statistics found). | A-r2-001 | primary-city matching tiers at the R2 merge (271 GASTAT primary cities ≥ 5,000, S-cp2-gastat-008, to register rows) | strictest first: judgement (screening/primary_match_decisions.yaml) → CP2 match (A-screen-038 tiers) carried by OSM object, or same name skeleton within 2 km for name-keyed rows → sweep already wrote the primary figure → relay bridge (identical figure, unique, in an archived citypopulation.de Census 2022 page; its Arabic name in the relay's region, then the same name in the full OSM place extract incl. suburb/neighbourhood inside the relay governorate, then the Arabic skeleton) → identical figure under another name (C-gastat-003/004) → Arabic consonant skeleton of the export spelling (≥ 3 consonants, unique, not shared by two primary cities) | rule | name-only matching without a size check (rejected: false matches across regions, e.g. Aljurn → الجعرانة in Makkah, though the relay places الجرن in Al-Ahsa); fuzzy string distance (rejected: the TA1 English spellings are machine transliterations) | Tariq, 31 Oct 2026 16:25 (prompts/R2.md addition 1: Arabic/English variants, transliteration, nearest OSM place, geo-verify); method choices by R2; results runs/r2/primary_match/primary_cities.csv. approval: Tariq, 31 Oct 2026 (CP3 4a). Extended at INTEGRATE_R2 (CP3 4a, 31 Oct 2026): the relay-bridge tier also reads the national citypopulation.de Census 2022 cities page (S-qassim-003; region from its region code; a row also on a regional page is dropped in favour of the regional row) and the OSM place polygons (ways/relations at their representative point, `osm-gcc-place-areas-extract-2026-10-14`; nodes first, polygons only where no node carries the name), and a same-name tie in one region goes to the unique highest place rank (A-r2-003); judgement decisions may point at an OSM place outside the register and the R1a universe | assumed | screening/primary_match.py |
| A-r2-002 | size plausibility band for a name match (size hint = the place's own figure, else its WorldPop grid figure, divided by the primary total) | 0.105–2.103 where a relay fixes the region; 0.370–1.157 for a name-only national match | ratio | 0.05–5.0 (A-screen-038's grid guard and a loose upper bound) | Derived: the 5th–95th percentiles and the interquartile range of the grid / census ratio over the 201 CP2 back-test cities (runs/gastat/primary/backtest_cities.csv; median 0.710, A-screen-039). approval: Tariq, 31 Oct 2026 (CP3 4a) | derived | screening/primary_match.py (GUARD, GUARD_NATIONAL) |
| A-r2-003 | tie-break when one relay name matches several OSM places in the relay region (no governorate on the relay, or several inside it) | the place with the unique highest OSM place rank (city > town > village > suburb/quarter > neighbourhood > hamlet > locality > isolated_dwelling/farm); no match if the top rank is shared | rule | no tie-break (R2: such cities stayed unmatched); nearest to the region centroid (rejected: no evidence) | Judgement (INTEGRATE_R2, CP3 4a, 31 Oct 2026): a Census 2022 city of 5,000+ is more likely the higher-ranked OSM place than a same-name hamlet or locality. Applied twice at INTEGRATE_R2: Alkalabiuh (village polygon الكلابيه over neighbourhood polygon الكلابية, both in Al-Ahsa) and Alramiluh (neighbourhood node in the oasis over a locality node at 21.95 N in the south of the governorate); Alqawz did not need it (the Makkah register holds one القوز, the town whose OSM 2010 tag 23,391 equals the relay's 2010 figure); results runs/r2/primary_match/primary_cities.csv | assumed | screening/primary_match.py (PLACE_RANK) |

| A-r6-106 | NSGA-II restart cap on the extended fast model: a run stops when the front is unchanged over 2 restarts (R6 rule, A-r6-013) **or** after 30 restarts, whichever comes first; the stop reason is recorded per run (`runs/r6/v3/ga/*/info.json`) | 30 | restarts | — | with 214 options (71 base + 143 variants) the non-dominated set keeps gaining near-equal capex/NPV points (325 → 637 → 787 → 976 points over the first 3 restarts of B1 seed 1, `logs/r6v3/ga-B1-s1.log`), so the R6 rule alone did not stop a run in 10 minutes; R6 (v2) runs needed 6–16 restarts (`runs/r6/ga/*/info.json`), so 30 is about twice the v2 maximum | assumed | cities/ksa/assumptions/r6v3_variants.yaml (search); optimise/variants.py (nsga2) |

| A-r6-133 | S6-L6 search: exact MILP (scipy/HiGHS) over the R6_V3 library options (71 searchable corridors, A-r6-008, and their 143 costed variants less the track demonstration; at most one option per corridor), connectivity by lazy node-separator cuts on the item graph (S1 components and options as nodes, arcs where stations meet the interchange rules), re-checked on the real graph. Objective lexicographic: capex P50 (uplifted, undiscounted, SAR m 2025) first, then the number of corridors. Ties: equal-capex swaps (within 0.5 SAR m) that stay feasible on the real graph. Next sets: each must drop at least one LINE corridor of every earlier set (station additions of equal capex are ties). | A-screen-031 | population basis precedence and the grid-only gate (CP2 P1a) | official (GASTAT, read directly = `verified`, or relayed = `secondary` + `primary_to_find`) > derived (corroborated) > grid (WorldPop, `pop_type: grid`, `pop_source_status: unverified`); a grid-only row has `tag_10k = true` only if A-screen-033 corroborates it within A-screen-032, otherwise `tag_10k = false` and `exclusion_reason` "grid-only population, uncorroborated". No correction (e.g. the A-screen-034 ratio) is ever applied to the base case | rule | alternatives: gate derived rows too; gate the 5k sensitivity too | Tariq's CP2 decision P1a, 31 Oct 2026 (docs/STATUS.md "CP2 decisions"): "GASTAT-relayed figures win where they exist; WorldPop fills gaps only, every gap row flagged `pop_type: grid`, `pop_source_status: unverified`. A grid-only settlement may pass the ≥ 10k test only if a second independent method ... corroborates it within 30 %". approval: Tariq, 31 Oct 2026 (CP2). Interpretation by CP2_PREP item 1 (assumed, confirm at CP3): (a) the gate applies to the ≥ 10k test only (A-screen-001); the 5k sensitivity (A-screen-002, `tag_5k`) is not gated, because the decision names only the 10k test; (b) derived rows are not gated (the decision gates grid-only rows); they are reported as corroborated / uncorroborated against the WorldPop grid; (c) a row carries one `pop_type`, so the precedence decides which figure a row holds (official over derived over grid), and the gate decides whether a grid figure counts. Addendum 2 (Tariq, 31 Oct 2026 15:25): the primary GASTAT Census 2022 TA1 exports (S-cp2-gastat-007/008) supersede the relayed file at governorate and city level; a settlement matched to a primary city of 5,000 or more is `official` + `verified` and passes the ≥ 10k test on that figure alone (no corroboration). | assumed | common/schemas.py (`RegionRow`, `corroborates`, `GRID_UNCORROBORATED`); screening/funnel.py (`population_basis`, `tag_10k_rule`, `long_list`); region-sweep and screening-funnel skills; prompts/REGION_*.md |
